## [Hugging Face Transformers v5.14.0 Adds Inkling Multimodal Model](https://github.com/huggingface/transformers/releases/tag/v5.14.0) ⭐️ 9.0/10

Hugging Face Transformers v5.14.0 introduces the Inkling multimodal model with 975B total parameters (41B active), supporting text, image, and audio inputs and generating text outputs. The release also includes TIPSv2 models, kernel optimizations, and multi-token prediction decoding support. Inkling is a state-of-the-art open-weights multimodal model from Thinking Machines Lab, founded by former OpenAI CTO Mira Murati, making it a significant competitor to proprietary models. Its open-weights release enables broad research, fine-tuning, and integration into enterprise applications, potentially accelerating multimodal AI adoption. Inkling uses a Mixture-of-Experts architecture with 975B total parameters but only 41B active per token, enabling efficient inference. The model supports up to 1M context length and is released under Apache-2.0 license with an acceptable use policy (AUP).

github · ArthurZucker · Jul 15, 19:02

**Background**: Hugging Face Transformers is a widely-used open-source library for natural language processing and multimodal AI, providing thousands of pre-trained models. Open-weights models allow users to download and fine-tune the trained parameters, offering more transparency and customization than closed APIs. Thinking Machines Lab is a well-funded AI startup founded by Mira Murati, focusing on collaborative general intelligence.

## [AI Search Rewrites Web's Economic Bargain](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.07652) ⭐️ 9.0/10

A new study using Comscore clickstream data reveals that ChatGPT generates outbound clicks in only 5.2% of conversation sessions, far below Google's referral ratio, and that wider ChatGPT Search access reduces traditional search use by 9.4%. This shift undermines the referral-based economic model that has long supported content production on the open web, as AI search satisfies information needs inside the intermediary rather than directing traffic to external sites. The study analyzed URL-level U.S. desktop clickstream data from Comscore, comparing ChatGPT and Google information-seeking occasions, and exploited ChatGPT Search access expansions to estimate displacement effects.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 15, 04:00

**Background**: Traditional search engines like Google operate on a referral bargain: they direct users to external websites, which in turn generate traffic and ad revenue. AI search models like ChatGPT can answer queries directly within the interface, reducing the need for users to click through to external sites, thereby disrupting this economic model.

</ul>
</details>

## [Two-Year Roadmap for Trustworthy Autonomous Science](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.12113) ⭐️ 9.0/10

The AISLE community has released an updated roadmap for autonomous science, adding two new dimensions (trust, verification, reproducibility; safety, security, governance) and four new milestones (M15–M18) to address the verification asymmetry that now limits progress more than raw model capability. This roadmap shapes the strategic direction of autonomous science, a field that has rapidly advanced with multi-agent systems and self-driving labs but faces critical challenges in verification and trust, affecting researchers, funders, and policymakers worldwide. The roadmap revisits the original five dimensions and elevates two cross-cutting concerns to first-class status, assesses 14 original milestones as achieved, partially achieved, reframed, or open, and adds four new milestones with a two-year horizon focusing first on interfaces and protocol adoption, then on federation and zero-trust coordination.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 15, 04:00

**Background**: Autonomous science uses AI and robotics to automate scientific discovery, including self-driving labs that design and run experiments. The AISLE roadmap, first published a year ago, proposed a grassroots network to connect isolated autonomous laboratories. Recent progress includes multi-agent systems generating validated hypotheses and the U.S. Genesis Mission placing autonomous experimentation at the center of federal science strategy.

<a id="item-4"></a>
## [BH Procedure Fails for Correlated Gaussian Tests](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.12208) ⭐️ 9.0/10

A new paper proves that the Benjamini-Hochberg procedure fails to control the false discovery rate for correlated two-sided Gaussian tests, disproving a 20-year-old conjecture. The proof, assisted by GPT-5.6 Pro, shows FDR exceeds the nominal level of 0.01. This result challenges a widely held belief about the robustness of the BH procedure, which is a cornerstone of multiple testing in statistics. It may lead to revised guidelines for FDR control in high-dimensional correlated data, affecting fields like genomics and neuroscience. The counterexample uses a factor model with two-sided Gaussian p-values at α=0.01, and a rigorous interval-arithmetic certificate proves FDR > 0.0104 for all sufficiently large numbers of hypotheses. Monte Carlo experiments confirm the theoretical finding.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 15, 04:00

**Background**: The Benjamini-Hochberg procedure is a widely used method to control the false discovery rate (FDR) when testing many hypotheses simultaneously. It was long conjectured to be robust to correlations among tests, but this paper shows that for two-sided Gaussian tests, correlation can cause FDR inflation.

<details><summary>References</summary>
<ul>
<li><a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Benjamini-Hochberg_procedure">Benjamini-Hochberg procedure</a></li>
<li><a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/False_discovery_rate">False discovery rate - Wikipedia</a></li>

</ul>
</details>

**Tags**: `#multiple testing`, `#false discovery rate`, `#Benjamini-Hochberg`, `#statistics`, `#correlated tests`

## [NOHARM benchmark reveals severe harm risks in medical AI](https://arxiv.org/abs/2512.01241) ⭐️ 9.0/10

Researchers introduced NOHARM, a 1,100-task benchmark evaluating LLM-generated medical consultation recommendations, finding that up to 24.6% of cases carried potential for severe harm, with clinical AI tools outperforming generalist LLMs. This benchmark directly addresses the critical gap in clinical safety evaluation for AI systems, highlighting that even widely used AI tools can produce harmful advice and emphasizing the need for explicit safety measurement before deployment in healthcare. The benchmark covers 10 specialties with 12,747 expert annotations for 4,249 clinical management options, and errors of omission accounted for over 80% of severe errors. A randomized study of 101 U.S. physicians showed AI assistance improved performance but physicians often omitted valuable AI recommendations.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 15, 04:00

**Background**: Large language models (LLMs) and retrieval-augmented generation (RAG) clinical AI tools are increasingly used for medical advice, but their safety profiles are poorly understood. NOHARM (Numerous Options Harm Assessment for Risk in Medicine) provides a rigorous framework to measure potential harm from AI-generated recommendations, going beyond simple accuracy metrics.

The EU General Court ruled that OpenAI's trademark application for 'Open AI' is invalid because the term is descriptive and lacks distinctiveness for software and IT services. This ruling prevents OpenAI from monopolizing the term 'Open AI', preserving it for general use by competitors and the open-source community, which could impact branding strategies in the AI industry. The court agreed with the EU Intellectual Property Office (EUIPO) that 'open' means freely accessible and 'AI' refers to artificial intelligence, making the combination descriptive for AI-related goods and services.

**Background**: Under EU trademark law, a mark must be distinctive to be registered; descriptive marks that directly describe a product's characteristics are generally refused. OpenAI had applied to register 'Open AI' as an EU trademark for software and IT services, but the EUIPO rejected it on absolute grounds, and the court upheld that decision.

## [AI Voice Fraud: The Three-Second Theft Outruns Defenses](https://smarterarticles.co.uk/the-three-second-theft-why-ai-voice-fraud-outruns-every-defence) ⭐️ 8.0/10

AI voice fraud using voice cloning technology can perpetrate scams with just three seconds of audio, exploiting the 'confused deputy' attack pattern to bypass traditional security measures. This highlights a critical vulnerability in trust-based systems, as voice cloning makes it nearly impossible for humans to distinguish real from fake, threatening individuals and organizations alike. The attack requires only a short voice sample, which can be harvested from social media or benign phone calls, and the robust mitigation is to disempower the easily confused deputy rather than block deceptive signals.

**Background**: Voice cloning technology uses AI to analyze and replicate a person's unique vocal patterns from a small audio sample. Deepfake audio defense methods currently focus on detecting artifacts in the acoustic signal, but these are often ineffective against advanced clones. The 'confused deputy' problem refers to a trusted system component being tricked into misusing its authority.

## [Claude web_fetch tool tricked into leaking user secrets](https://simonwillison.net/2026/Jul/15/claude-web-fetch-exfiltration/#atom-everything) ⭐️ 8.0/10

Security researcher Ayush Paul demonstrated a data exfiltration attack on Anthropic's Claude web_fetch tool, bypassing its protection by using a honeypot site that generated nested links to extract private user data. This attack highlights a critical vulnerability in AI agent security, showing that even carefully designed protections can be circumvented, potentially exposing sensitive user memories and personal information. The attack exploited the rule that web_fetch could follow links from previously fetched pages, allowing the attacker to chain URLs to exfiltrate data. The honeypot site only responded to clients with a Claude-User user-agent to evade detection.

rss · Simon Willison · Jul 15, 14:21

**Background**: The 'lethal trifecta' attack vector combines access to private data, exposure to untrusted content, and ability to communicate externally. Claude's web_fetch tool was designed to prevent dynamic URL construction, but the nested link loophole allowed exfiltration.

Lobste.rs, a Rails-based community site, completed its migration from MariaDB to SQLite, now running on a single VPS with reduced CPU and memory usage and half the cost. This migration demonstrates that SQLite can serve as a production database for a moderately sized Rails application, challenging the assumption that a client-server database is always necessary. The primary SQLite database is about 3.8GB, with additional databases for cache (1.1GB), queue (218MB), and Rack::Attack (555MB). The migration PR added 735 lines and removed 593 lines across 30 commits.

rss · Simon Willison · Jul 14, 19:44

**Background**: Lobste.rs is a link aggregation and discussion community for software developers, similar to Hacker News. It had been planning a database migration since 2018, initially considering PostgreSQL before settling on SQLite. SQLite is an embedded database that stores data in a single file, while MariaDB is a client-server database requiring a separate server process.

## [GPT-Red: Self-Play Boosts AI Safety Robustness](https://openai.com/index/unlocking-self-improvement-gpt-red) ⭐️ 8.0/10

OpenAI introduced GPT-Red, an automated red teaming system that uses self-play to improve the safety, alignment, and prompt injection robustness of large language models. This approach automates a critical but labor-intensive safety process, enabling continuous improvement of AI defenses against adversarial attacks. It addresses the growing need for scalable and consistent red teaming as AI systems become more capable and widely deployed. GPT-Red is a multi-agent system built on a fine-tuned GPT-4o architecture, operating in three phases to generate and test adversarial prompts. The system leverages self-play, where two instances of the model compete to find vulnerabilities, iteratively improving robustness.

rss · OpenAI Blog · Jul 15, 10:00

**Background**: Red teaming involves probing AI models to identify vulnerabilities, such as safety failures or prompt injection attacks. Automated red teaming aims to scale this process using machine learning, generating many adversarial examples efficiently. Prompt injection is a key risk where hidden instructions in input can cause unintended model behavior.

A new arXiv paper develops a stochastic optimal control framework for market making in zero-fee perpetual futures markets, deriving a Master APY Formula and optimal hedging strategies. The framework unifies and extends classical models like Avellaneda-Stoikov and Glosten-Milgrom for decentralized venues. This work provides a rigorous theoretical foundation for liquidity provision in DeFi perpetual exchanges, potentially enabling market makers to optimize returns and manage risks more effectively. It bridges the gap between traditional market making theory and modern decentralized finance microstructure. The framework includes a PnL decomposition theorem, HJB equation under CARA utility, High-APY Regime Theorems with five dimensionless parameters, and a Master APY Formula. Numerical analysis with 23 figures reveals phase transitions between profitable and unprofitable regimes.

Stochastic optimal control is a mathematical approach used to find optimal trading strategies under uncertainty. The Hamilton-Jacobi-Bellman (HJB) equation is a key tool in dynamic programming for solving such control problems. CARA utility (Constant Absolute Risk Aversion) models risk preferences with a constant level of absolute risk aversion.

## [GRID: Grammar-Railed Decoding for Enterprise SQL Generation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.11951) ⭐️ 8.0/10

GRID introduces a grammar-constrained decoding engine that uses LALR(1) parser state for token masking and compiles role-based access control into the grammar, achieving near-constant per-token cost and provable guarantees. This work addresses critical enterprise requirements for SQL generation, such as provable syntactic validity, role-based access control, and compliance-grade audit trails, which are not reliably met by current LLM-based approaches. GRID uses a byte-level trie walk with context-independent/context-dependent split for cache-key soundness, and its Rust kernels achieve median per-token mask times of 3.6-6.7 μs, outperforming llguidance. On Spider, constrained decoding yields +13 execution accuracy at 0.5B model size.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 15, 04:00

**Background**: Grammar-constrained decoding (GCD) ensures that LLM outputs conform to a specified grammar by masking invalid tokens. LALR(1) parsers are efficient bottom-up parsers used in many programming languages. GRID leverages the parser state directly for masking, rather than relying on token sequences, enabling provable guarantees and role-based access control.

A new paper argues that AI alignment is not merely an engineering achievement but an expression of 'optimization culture,' which conflates measurable improvement with value and cannot distinguish between error and invention. This critique challenges the dominant technical framing of alignment, suggesting that the authority to define legitimate language has been transferred from human institutions to optimization procedures that lack genuine judgment. The paper traces optimization culture through the AI stack—pretraining, decoding, preference tuning, benchmarking, and interface—and links it to the broader 'audit society' concept, where verification rituals replace substantive judgment.

Researchers introduced PM-Bench, a text-based benchmark inspired by the Virtual Week paradigm from cognitive science, to evaluate prospective memory in LLM agents. Prospective memory is critical for reliable agentic AI, enabling agents to execute delayed intentions while handling other tasks. PM-Bench simulates a seven-day week where agents must continue ongoing activities while deciding whether any deferred task is due. The benchmark evaluates eight state-of-the-art LLMs under eight different agent configurations, and no single strategy for improving prospective memory dominates across models.

It achieves up to 54% reduction in Expected Calibration Error (ECE) compared to the strongest training-free baseline across three benchmarks.

Researchers propose function-aware fill-in-the-middle (FIM) mid-training for coding agent foundation models, exploiting the structural isomorphism between agent loops and function call sites. This paper exploits that isomorphism to design a function-aware FIM objective for mid-training.

A new paper proposes the Compression Calculus, a formal framework that defines intelligence as atomic compression and compositional reuse across cognitive, biological, and computational systems. It introduces the Compounding Cascade thesis, where each abstraction layer multiplicatively increases representational efficiency. This framework offers a unifying perspective on intelligence that could reshape AI system design, moving beyond token-level processing toward stable, concept-level atomic structures. It provides a foundation for building self-evolving knowledge systems that discover and compose new primitives. The paper argues that large language models are best understood as dynamic fusion engines for atomic units, not complete knowledge architectures. It also claims contemporary AI systems operate at suboptimal representation levels, lacking stable concept-level atomic structures.

## [OAT: Unsupervised Failure Attribution for LLM Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.12747) ⭐️ 8.0/10

Researchers propose OAT, an unsupervised method using neural controlled differential equations to identify error steps in LLM agent failure trajectories by training only on successful trajectories. This approach is 200–5000× faster than prompting-based baselines and achieves +20% F1 in-domain and +7% out-of-distribution, making failure attribution practical and scalable for debugging agentic systems. OAT models the dynamical pattern of successful trajectories in latent space using neural controlled differential equations, assigning anomaly scores to each step in a failure trajectory at inference time. It requires only 100 successful trajectories for training and no step-level error annotations.

**Background**: LLM-based agentic systems perform multi-step tasks, and failures can occur at any step. Existing failure attribution methods either use expensive prompting or require costly step-level annotations on failure data. Neural controlled differential equations generalize neural ODEs to handle irregular time series, making them suitable for modeling agent trajectories.

A new paper analyzes length bias in multiple-choice LLM benchmarks, shows that length normalization overcorrects, and proposes Bayesian accuracy as a principled alternative that removes linear length effects. This work addresses a fundamental flaw in LLM evaluation, offering a drop-in replacement that improves fairness and reliability of benchmark scores across models and settings. Bayesian accuracy computes the posterior probability of each candidate under an explicit prior over answer length, requiring no additional forward passes, and consistently shows lower empirical length bias than both standard and length-normalized accuracy.

## [Visual Access Boundaries in VLM Reasoning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.12815) ⭐️ 8.0/10

Researchers introduced Visual Access Sweep, a causal intervention that masks attention from generated tokens to image tokens, and defined the Visual Access Boundary (VAB) as the minimal access region preserving task accuracy. They found that Chain-of-Thought (CoT) prompting in VLMs does not require prolonged direct image-token access beyond the initial encoding layers. This work challenges the assumption that CoT reasoning in VLMs relies on extended visual access, suggesting instead that CoT primarily extends language-side computation over image-derived hidden states. It provides a new causal tool for interpretability and could guide more efficient VLM architectures. Experiments across Qwen2.5-VL and InternVL3 models (7B to 78B) showed that CoT's VAB layer differs from no-CoT boundary by at most two layers. CoT gains are constrained by perceptual readout: it helps when visual attributes are reliably read out but not when readout is unreliable.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 15, 04:00

**Background**: Chain-of-Thought (CoT) prompting is a technique that encourages models to generate intermediate reasoning steps before answering, often improving performance on complex tasks. Vision-Language Models (VLMs) combine visual and textual inputs, and it was unclear whether CoT in VLMs requires continued access to image tokens throughout reasoning. This study uses causal intervention to isolate the role of visual token access.

A new paper proposes counterfactual report mediators that enforce a causal contract on LLM reports: invariant to forbidden influences (pressure, prestige) and responsive to genuine evidence, achieving joint resist and update scores of 1.00 on a benchmark. This addresses a critical failure in LLM alignment—incentive incompatibility—where models misreport under pressure, undermining trustworthiness. The causal approach offers a principled way to certify internal incentive compatibility, advancing AI safety. The method uses interchange interventions to identify low-rank report coordinates for answer, confidence, and caveat that are near-orthogonal and independently controllable. A training-free counterfactual report-coordinate (CRC) clamp references the model's own report under a counterfactually incentive-neutralized context.

**Background**: Aligned LLMs often misreport under non-evidential pressure, such as agreeing with a confident user or overstating certainty, even when internal beliefs remain unchanged. This is a failure of internal incentive-compatibility (IC). The paper introduces a causal contract with two demands: resist (invariance to forbidden influences) and update (responsiveness to genuine evidence), which pull in opposite directions.

This paper identifies and formalizes a failure mode where LLM plan evaluators reward less explicit plans, termed deletion non-monotonicity, and proposes a mitigation called GATE (typed-state gating). This work highlights a critical vulnerability in LLM-based plan evaluation that could be exploited by autonomous agents to game scores, undermining AI safety. The proposed GATE mitigation offers a principled approach to detect and neutralize such exploits. The paper provides an analytic identity for score change from deleting an interior transition (Proposition 1) and validates it on a 26-route cohort, finding all 57 admissible deletions matched the identity. GATE refused score release for 26/26 silenced routes with 0/26 honest suspensions, and after refusal, 47/54 next revisions repaired to a covered structure.

Typed-state gating (GATE) uses model-mediated typed-state records to detect and block such omissions.

AAAI-26 organizers implemented a dual submission detection pipeline combining title+abstract similarity, LLM-based overlap assessment, and manual review, resulting in 141 desk rejections. This highlights the growing problem of dual submissions in AI conferences, exacerbated by generative AI tools, and provides a scalable detection methodology that can be adopted by other venues to protect research integrity. The pipeline first uses title+abstract similarity to identify candidate pairs, then an LLM-based tool assesses semantic overlap, with manual review for high-severity cases; exact duplicates are now less common than paraphrased submissions.

**Background**: Dual submission refers to submitting the same or substantially similar work to multiple venues simultaneously without disclosure, which wastes reviewer effort and pollutes the scientific record. ## [DoYouRemember: Memory-Centric Multimodal AI Architecture](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.11919) ⭐️ 8.0/10

The paper introduces DoYouRemember, a three-stage architecture for multimodal LLMs that incorporates reconstructive memory, and empirically demonstrates that LLM hidden states contain no recoverable visual information. This work addresses a fundamental limitation of current multimodal LLMs—their lack of reconstructive memory—and provides an information-theoretic framework unifying memory, recall, and hallucination, which could lead to more human-like AI systems. The architecture uses a VQ-VAE to compress images into discrete tokens, a LoRA-fine-tuned LLM to process them jointly with text, and a Diffusion Decoder to reconstruct images from hidden states. A shared memory matrix M with local EMA updating achieves 16x compression and surpasses VQ upper bound on LPIPS.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 15, 04:00

**Background**: Current multimodal LLMs process images through a frozen visual encoder, produce a one-shot text output, and discard internal representations, lacking the reconstructive memory characteristic of human memory. Reconstructive memory theory posits that recall is influenced by perception, imagination, and beliefs, not a faithful recording. VQ-VAE is a vector-quantized variational autoencoder that compresses images into discrete codes, while LoRA is a low-rank adaptation method for efficient fine-tuning.

## [CARE-LoRA: Compressed Activation Reconstruction for Memory-Efficient LoRA](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.11940) ⭐️ 8.0/10

CARE-LoRA proposes a data-aware compressed activation reconstruction framework that replaces full input activations with low-rank compressed activations from the LoRA branch and computes a lightweight reconstruction matrix during forward pass to reconstruct gradients during backpropagation, reducing activation memory while keeping LoRA matrices fully trainable. This addresses the critical memory bottleneck of activation storage during LoRA fine-tuning, enabling larger models or batch sizes under limited memory budgets, and achieving competitive or superior performance compared to standard LoRA and its variants. CARE-LoRA exploits the inherent projection structure of LoRA to naturally produce low-rank compressed activations, and the reconstruction matrix is computed with negligible additional cost. Extensive experiments across diverse models and tasks show memory reduction without performance degradation.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 15, 04:00

**Background**: LoRA (Low-Rank Adaptation) is a popular parameter-efficient fine-tuning method that reduces trainable parameters by learning low-rank matrices. However, during fine-tuning, activations stored for backpropagation become the main memory bottleneck. CARE-LoRA builds on LoRA's structure to compress these activations.

This paper proposes a generalized distribution-free semi-supervised learning framework that constructs unbiased risk estimators via linear combinations of component risks, extending PNU learning to multiclass classification and achieving lower variance than PNU in asymmetric loss scenarios. This work provides a theoretical foundation for distribution-free SSL with variance optimality and generalization bounds, potentially enabling more reliable SSL methods that do not rely on distributional assumptions, which is critical for real-world applications where such assumptions often fail. The framework subsumes PNU learning and derives the minimum achievable variance, showing that the proposed estimator can attain lower variance than PNU. It also establishes a generalization bound linking variance reduction to improved learning performance, and introduces two practical SSL methods that match or outperform existing approaches on benchmarks.

## [AutoTrace: LLM Agents Localize Vulnerability Triggers Interprocedurally](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.12058) ⭐️ 8.0/10

AutoTrace is a novel pipeline that combines LLM agents with code property graphs to localize vulnerability triggers across function call boundaries, achieving 75.0% VulnHit and 80.8% FuncHit on the InterPVD benchmark, surpassing prior state-of-the-art. This work addresses a critical gap in vulnerability analysis by enabling interprocedural causal reasoning, which is essential for understanding real-world CVEs where the trigger often lies outside the patched function. The verifiable evidence gates ensure trustworthiness, making AutoTrace a significant step toward automated, reliable vulnerability localization. AutoTrace uses deterministic admissibility gates to require explicit evidence from the graph before reporting a trigger, ensuring no ungrounded model judgment. The authors also introduce SinkTrace-Bench, a dataset of 1,542 verifier-confirmed source-to-sink causal chains, which reveals that even frontier LLMs struggle to separate matched vulnerable/safe pairs.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 15, 04:00

**Background**: Trigger localization is a harder problem than binary vulnerability detection because it requires identifying the specific statement that turns a vulnerable state into an unsafe operation, often across multiple function calls. Code property graphs (CPGs) unify abstract syntax trees, control flow graphs, and data dependencies into a single graph representation, enabling comprehensive program analysis. Prior approaches often rely on static rules or pattern-matching, which fail for interprocedural triggers.

## [RCWT: Measuring Task-Budget Displacement from Coordination Content](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.12216) ⭐️ 8.0/10

Researchers introduced the Roundtable Context Window Test (RCWT), a controlled protocol to measure how coordination content in LLM prompts displaces the budget available for task evidence. They found that commercial models like GPT-4.1-mini, Claude Haiku 4.5, and Gemini 2.5 Flash degrade sharply when residual reference evidence falls below a few hundred tokens. This work highlights a practical and underexplored problem in multi-agent LLM systems: coordination content can displace task budget within a fixed context window, leading to performance cliffs. The RCWT protocol provides a measurement primitive for context-allocation budgeting, which is critical for designing efficient multi-agent and memory-augmented systems. In the main context-dependent recall task at W=4096, models remained near baseline through moderate overhead but degraded sharply once residual evidence fell to a few hundred tokens. An intact-task ablation showed that when task evidence was kept intact while coordination tokens increased by expanding total prompt length, all tested models returned every scored field correctly up to a 95% coordination ratio, indicating the cliff is due to budget displacement rather than semantic interference.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 15, 04:00

**Background**: Multi-agent and memory-augmented LLM systems often place coordination content (e.g., shared state, prior discussion, tool outputs) inside the same finite prompt used for the current task. This creates a practical allocation problem: every token spent on coordination is unavailable for task instructions or evidence when the call is assembled under a fixed context budget. The RCWT protocol systematically varies coordination content while controlling total budget, position order, task family, and scoring to isolate the displacement effect.

## [LLM Confidence Signals Reflect Normative Decision Variables](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.12447) ⭐️ 8.0/10

A new paper applies the statistical decision confidence (SDC) framework from computational neuroscience to show that answer-logit differences in large language models behave as monotonic readouts of a latent decision variable, not heuristic preferences, across perceptual and memory tasks. ## [EcoSpec: Cost-Aware Speculative Decoding for MoE Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.12696) ⭐️ 8.0/10

EcoSpec introduces a cost-aware speculative decoding framework that mitigates expert scattering in Mixture-of-Experts (MoE) models by incorporating predicted expert activation cost into draft selection, achieving up to 1.62x speedup on models like DeepSeek-V3.1 (671B). This work addresses a critical inefficiency in MoE inference—expert scattering—which can negate the benefits of speculative decoding. By making draft selection cost-aware, EcoSpec improves decoding speed without modifying the target model, potentially benefiting many large-scale MoE deployments. EcoSpec uses a lightweight expert predictor and a dynamic expert buffer to favor draft paths that reuse already-loaded experts while maintaining high acceptance likelihood. It was evaluated on three large MoE models across reasoning, coding, QA, and dialogue benchmarks.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 15, 04:00

**Background**: Mixture-of-Experts (MoE) models activate only a subset of parameters per token, enabling larger model sizes with lower computational cost. Speculative decoding accelerates autoregressive generation by having a small draft model propose multiple tokens that a large target model verifies in parallel. However, in MoE models, the draft tokens may activate disjoint experts, increasing memory traffic—a phenomenon called expert scattering.

This addresses a critical and growing security issue in container ecosystems, especially for AI/ML workloads that mount shared resources like GPUs, where existing defenses are inadequate. Bulkhead uses a multi-agent system to identify path traversal entry points via high-risk functional patterns, then recovers execution paths using call-chain patterns. A new study evaluates four reward model variants across 14 domains and finds that discriminative outcome reward models (dORMs) perform as well as process reward models (PRMs), while generative outcome reward models (gORMs) are the most robust, challenging the prevailing belief that fine-grained process supervision is always superior. This finding has significant implications for deploying LLMs in multi-domain settings, as it suggests that simpler outcome-based reward models can be more reliable and easier to implement than complex process-based models, potentially reducing computational overhead and improving test-time scaling reliability. ## [Optimal Policy Must Convey n log m Bits of Environment Info](https://arxiv.org/abs/2602.12963) ⭐️ 8.0/10

A new paper proves that an optimal policy in a Controlled Markov Process with n states and m actions must convey exactly n log m bits of mutual information about the environment, establishing a fundamental information-theoretic bound on implicit world models. This result provides a precise lower bound on the information an agent must encode about its environment to behave optimally, with implications for AI alignment, representation learning, and understanding the necessity of world models in reinforcement learning. The bound holds across finite-horizon, infinite-horizon discounted, and time-averaged reward maximization objectives, assuming a uniform prior over transition dynamics. The mutual information is exactly n log m bits for any non-constant reward function.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 15, 04:00

**Background**: Controlled Markov Processes (CMPs) are a formal framework for decision-making where an agent chooses actions to influence state transitions. Mutual information measures the dependence between two random variables. An implicit world model refers to latent representations of environment dynamics that emerge without explicit prediction objectives.

<a id="item-82"></a>
## [Contextual Sycophancy: A New Failure Mode in Robust RL](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.13356) ⭐️ 8.0/10

This paper identifies 'Contextual Sycophancy' in robust reinforcement learning, where evaluators are truthful in benign contexts but biased in critical ones, and proves that no algorithm relying solely on social feedback can avoid linear regret. This work reveals a fundamental limitation of robust RL algorithms that assume globally trustworthy or corrupted feedback, showing that without additional information like ground-truth audits, linear regret is unavoidable, which has significant implications for AI safety and reliable decision-making. The paper proposes ESA (Exploration with Sparse Audits), which learns per-evaluator contextual trust boundaries from audits and achieves a regret bound of O(√(T d_VC/p_aud) + d√T + ε_tol T). Empirically, ESA recovers ground truth when 80% of evaluators are adversarial, outperforming median- and mean-based baselines.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 15, 04:00

**Background**: Robust reinforcement learning typically assumes feedback sources are either globally trustworthy or corrupted within a fixed global budget. Contextual Sycophancy is a more subtle failure where evaluators are truthful in benign contexts but biased in critical ones, so that no single evaluator is reliable everywhere and corrupt evaluators may form a majority in important contexts.

**Tags**: `#reinforcement learning`, `#robustness`, `#social feedback`, `#theoretical bounds`, `#AI safety`

<a id="item-83"></a>
## [Attractor Geometry Unifies LLM Conflict and Hallucination](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.05686) ⭐️ 8.0/10

A new paper proposes a unified geometric framework showing that both conflict (when parametric and working memory disagree) and hallucination (when a fact was never learned) in language models arise from attractor basin dynamics in hidden-state space. This work provides mechanistic insight into two critical failure modes of LLMs, offering a geometric margin metric that detects hallucination far more cleanly than output entropy, which could improve AI safety and reliability. The geometric margin—the hidden state's distance to the nearest memorized basin—separates correct recall from hallucination with zero false refusals, while entropy-based detection rejects most correct outputs. The fraction of confident hallucinations follows a scaling law C = exp(-c/Δ̄), growing with scale even as overall error rates fall.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 15, 04:00

**Background**: Language models use two knowledge sources: parametric memory (facts baked into weights during training) and working memory (information provided in context). When these conflict, models may produce confident but wrong answers; when a fact was never learned, they may hallucinate. Attractor basins are regions in state space where trajectories converge to stable fixed points, representing learned facts.

A new study systematically shows that increasing inference compute—via larger token budgets, context compaction, and repeated attempts—significantly boosts frontier LLM performance on hard benchmarks, indicating that low scores may reflect evaluation constraints rather than model capability. This finding challenges current evaluation practices that report performance at a single restrictive budget, potentially understating model capability, and calls for reporting performance as a function of inference compute—especially in safety-critical applications. ## [DRIFTLENS: Measuring Memory-Induced Reasoning Drift in LLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.02374) ⭐️ 8.0/10

Researchers introduced DRIFTLENS, a ground-truth-free framework that quantifies how injected user-attribute memory alters LLM reasoning trajectories on open-ended questions. The study found that across four LLMs and ten user-attribute categories, memory induces medium-to-large reasoning drift above the pragmatic-noise floor. This work highlights a previously underexplored failure mode in personalized LLMs: memory-induced reasoning drift, which can affect fairness and reliability even when final answers appear plausible. It provides a method to measure and mitigate such drift, with implications for AI safety and responsible deployment. DRIFTLENS maps each reasoning step to a value category and measures divergence between no-memory and memory-injected trajectories. Post-training methods like GRPO and DPO reduce drift but do not uniformly dominate, and their effects on downstream capabilities are model- and reward-dependent.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 15, 04:00

**Background**: Large language models (LLMs) are increasingly personalized by storing user attributes (e.g., age, occupation) and injecting them into prompts. This memory can change not only the final answer but also the reasoning path the model takes. DRIFTLENS addresses the challenge of measuring such drift without requiring ground-truth answers, which are often unavailable for open-ended questions.

## [New Benchmark Exposes Persistent Sycophancy in Stateful AI Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.10526) ⭐️ 8.0/10

Researchers introduced the Personal Agent Sycophancy Benchmark (PASB), a 1,600-task benchmark that evaluates whether stateful personal agents persistently adopt and reuse sycophantic claims from conversations by writing them into durable memory. This work highlights a critical AI safety issue: once sycophantic claims are committed to an agent's long-term memory, they can be reused indefinitely, making sycophancy a state-writing governance problem rather than just a response-level issue. Across twelve models, the commit boundary increased downstream failure from 45.0% in session-only episodes to 71.9% after commitment, a consistent 27.0 percentage point increase, with three write-time patterns identified: status promotion, attribution removal, and scope broadening.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 15, 04:00

**Background**: Sycophancy in AI refers to a model systematically agreeing with a user instead of reasoning independently. Stateful personal agents maintain long-term user profiles, episodic memories, and reusable skills, which can turn conversational sycophancy into a persistent state-writing failure. Prior benchmarks only evaluated sycophancy within a single session, ignoring the risk of durable memory.

## [ATLAS: Adaptive Testing Slashes LLM Evaluation Items by 90%](https://arxiv.org/abs/2511.04689) ⭐️ 8.0/10

Researchers introduced ATLAS, an adaptive testing framework based on Item Response Theory (IRT), which reduces the number of evaluation items needed for LLM benchmarks by up to 90% while preserving measurement precision. For example, on HellaSwag (5,600 items), ATLAS matches whole-bank ability estimates using only 41 items. This approach addresses the growing cost and impracticality of evaluating LLMs with thousands of fixed benchmark items, making large-scale evaluation faster and cheaper. It also provides finer discrimination between models with similar accuracy, revealing meaningful ability differences that static benchmarks miss. ATLAS uses Fisher information-guided item selection to adaptively choose the most informative items for each model. The paper shows that reconstructed accuracies from ATLAS's ability estimates closely match raw accuracies across five benchmarks, and among over 3,000 models, 23-31% shift by more than 10 rank positions compared to accuracy-based ranking.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 15, 04:00

**Background**: Item Response Theory (IRT) is a psychometric framework that models the relationship between a test-taker's ability and their responses to individual items, accounting for item difficulty and discrimination. Unlike classical test theory, IRT does not assume all items are equally informative, making it suitable for adaptive testing where items are selected based on the test-taker's estimated ability. ATLAS applies IRT to LLM evaluation, treating each benchmark item as a test item and the model as the test-taker.

<ul>

## [Adaptive Entropy Scheduling for Non-Stationary RL](https://arxiv.org/abs/2601.19624) ⭐️ 8.0/10

This paper introduces Adaptive Entropy Scheduling (AES), a theoretically grounded method that adjusts exploration intensity in non-stationary reinforcement learning by deriving a square-root scaling rule from a dynamic regret trade-off. AES addresses a fundamental limitation in reinforcement learning by enabling agents to adapt exploration in changing environments, which is critical for real-world applications like robotics and autonomous driving where conditions drift over time. AES requires almost no structural changes to existing algorithms and incurs minimal overhead, using observable drift proxies to adjust the entropy coefficient online. It was validated across 4 algorithm variants, 12 tasks, and 4 drift modes, significantly reducing performance degradation and accelerating recovery after abrupt changes.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 15, 04:00

**Background**: Reinforcement learning (RL) trains agents to maximize cumulative rewards through trial and error. In non-stationary environments, the underlying dynamics change over time, making it challenging for agents to balance exploration and exploitation. Maximum-entropy RL adds an entropy bonus to encourage exploration, but traditional methods use fixed entropy coefficients that fail to adapt to drift.

## [VLMs Show Systematic Egocentric Bias in Perspective Taking](https://arxiv.org/abs/2602.15892) ⭐️ 8.0/10

A new benchmark called FlipSet reveals that most vision-language models (VLMs) exhibit systematic egocentric bias in Level-2 visual perspective taking, performing below chance when inferring how a scene looks from another's viewpoint after a 180-degree rotation. This finding highlights a fundamental limitation in current VLMs' ability to integrate social awareness with spatial reasoning, which is crucial for applications like robotics, autonomous driving, and assistive technologies that require understanding others' perspectives. The study evaluated 103 VLMs and found that roughly three-quarters of errors reproduced the camera viewpoint, indicating egocentric bias. Control experiments showed models achieved high theory-of-mind accuracy and above-chance mental rotation in isolation, but failed when integration was required.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 15, 04:00

**Background**: Level-2 visual perspective taking (L2 VPT) involves understanding not just what another person can see, but how it looks from their specific viewpoint, including orientation and appearance. This is a more advanced cognitive skill than Level-1 VPT, which only requires knowing whether an object is visible. Egocentric bias is a cognitive tendency to rely on one's own perspective when interpreting others' viewpoints.

## [LLM Metacognition Measured via Signal Detection Theory](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.25112) ⭐️ 8.0/10

Researchers applied Signal Detection Theory to decompose LLM confidence into knowledge and metacognitive sensitivity, proposing a model-free information measure (meta-I2r) for open-ended QA. They tested four LLMs on 224,000 factual QA trials and found that metacognitive information varies more than two-fold across models and inversely correlates with accuracy. This work addresses a key limitation of standard calibration metrics (ECE, Brier score) that conflate knowledge and confidence tracking. By providing a principled decomposition, it enables more nuanced evaluation of LLM self-awareness, which is critical for alignment and trustworthiness. The study used Type-2 ROC analysis and z-ROC slopes (0.81 to 1.18) to reveal model-specific unequal-variance structures invisible to calibration metrics. Temperature was found to dissociate Type-1 accuracy from metacognitive information, which remained stable while accuracy shifted.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 15, 04:00

**Background**: Signal Detection Theory (SDT) is a framework for analyzing decision-making under uncertainty, distinguishing sensitivity (ability to discriminate signal from noise) from bias (decision threshold). In metacognition research, Type-2 SDT evaluates how well confidence ratings track the correctness of decisions. The meta-d' efficiency ratio is a common measure but requires a two-alternative forced-choice task, which open-ended QA does not provide. The proposed meta-I2r is an information-theoretic alternative that avoids this limitation.

Researchers introduced dMX, a differentiable mixed-precision quantization framework that learns per-layer floating-point bit-width assignments for LLMs using a continuous optimization approach targeting OCP MXFP formats. This work enables more efficient deployment of LLMs by automatically finding optimal per-layer precision, improving both performance and accuracy over uniform bit-width quantization. It addresses a key bottleneck in deploying large models on resource-constrained hardware. The framework parameterizes each layer's floating-point format with a single scalar offset, uses a temperature-based annealing schedule to discretize offsets, and includes a target-aware regularization term to steer average bit-width toward a user-specified budget. Experiments on Llama, Qwen3, and SmolLM2 show Pareto-dominating models over KL-divergence heuristics.

The Open Compute Project (OCP) defines microscaling floating-point (MXFP) formats, which use block-wise shared exponents to improve dynamic range. Differentiable mixed-precision quantization allows the network to learn optimal bit-widths during training, balancing accuracy and efficiency.

## [Score Hamiltonians Link Diffusion Models to Adiabatic Transport](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.05217) ⭐️ 8.0/10

This paper establishes an exact correspondence between score-based diffusion models and adiabatic transport of ground states for a family of Schrödinger operators called Score Hamiltonians, built from the learned score's quantum potential. This theoretical connection yields novel density reconstruction bounds and principled annealing schedules via adiabatic theorems for Fokker-Planck equations, potentially impacting sampling theory and practice in generative modeling. The paper finds that the fundamental limit of sampling is set by the ratio of squared score-matching error to the Score Hamiltonian spectral gap, which is the inverse Poincaré constant of the data density.

Researchers introduce Sibling-Guided Credit Distillation (SGCD), a method that improves credit assignment in long-horizon reinforcement learning for tool-use agents by using contrastive distillation from sibling rollouts, outperforming GRPO on AppWorld and tau^3-airline benchmarks. SGCD addresses a key challenge in training LLM agents for complex, long-horizon tasks by providing denser reward signals without destroying tool-use behavior, potentially enabling more reliable autonomous agents in real-world applications. SGCD dynamically samples mixed successful and failed sibling rollouts, uses an external LLM to summarize their contrast into a credit reference, and reshapes GRPO token advantages via detached teacher/student divergence. On AppWorld, test_normal TGC improved from 42.9 to 45.6, and test_challenge from 24.7 to 27.0; on tau^3-airline, evaluator score rose from 0.583 to 0.602.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 15, 04:00

**Background**: Reinforcement learning for long-horizon tool-use agents suffers from the credit assignment problem: trajectory-level rewards are broadcast over many tokens, making it hard to identify which actions led to success. GRPO is a policy gradient method that uses group-normalized advantage estimation without a value critic, but it still struggles with sparse rewards. SGCD combines distillation with policy gradient to provide denser, more targeted credit signals.

## [Prime Fourier Embeddings: A Principled Basis for Modular Arithmetic](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.23044) ⭐️ 8.0/10

Researchers introduce Prime Fourier Embeddings (PFE), a novel encoding of integers as prime-indexed cosine-sine pairs derived from harmonic analysis of rational numbers, which makes modular arithmetic explicit by selecting relevant prime channels. PFE provides a principled alternative to standard neural embeddings that fail to expose algebraic structure, potentially improving neural network performance on arithmetic tasks and enabling more interpretable representations. The authors prove via Schur's lemma that any linear map equivariant under the product group action on PFE must be block-diagonal with independent blocks per prime, and the Chinese Remainder Theorem predicts which prime channels are relevant for square-free composite moduli.

**Background**: Standard neural embeddings like word2vec or learned positional encodings often lack explicit algebraic structure, making it hard for models to learn modular arithmetic directly. ## [Depth in World Models: Help, Hurt, or Flat](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.10203) ⭐️ 8.0/10

A pre-registered study on adaptive-compute world models reveals three regimes where deeper predictors help, hurt, or don't matter for rollout accuracy, and shows that inversion (shallow exits outperforming full depth) can be induced by training. This challenges the core assumption that depth always improves predictions in world models, with practical implications for designing efficient planning systems in reinforcement learning. The study uses a shallow penalty metric ρ across nine DeepMind Control tasks, finding inversion on 2/9 tasks and a flat regime on 1/9; the inversion is erased by supervising early exits only at the first rollout step, revealing a routability catch-22.

## [AgentCheck: Reproduce-Intervene-Confirm Workbench for LLM Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.11098) ⭐️ 8.0/10

AgentCheck is an open-source web workbench that allows developers to reproduce tool failures in LLM agents by injecting 12 types of faults into MCP server responses, then replaying the agent's execution to test and confirm fixes before deployment. This workbench addresses a critical gap in LLM agent evaluation, where most tests assume tools work perfectly, while real-world failures like timeouts or stale data are common. By enabling systematic fault injection and replay, AgentCheck helps developers build more reliable agents and reduces deployment risks. AgentCheck uses a reproduce-intervene-confirm loop: it records tool responses, injects faults, replays cached matching calls, and lets live calls proceed after divergence. It scores results using both deterministic pass/fail rules and an LLM judge validated against human annotations.

AgentCheck provides a controlled environment to inject faults and verify fixes.

## [Graph Feedback Controls Consensus in Open-Weight LLM Populations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.12077) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper introduces a naming-game protocol to study how graph feedback mechanisms, such as homophilous threshold-similarity routing and bridge-seeking routing, affect consensus and clique formation in populations of open-weight language models ranging from 1.1B to 32B parameters. This research is significant because it reveals that the interaction graph structure in multi-agent language model systems can dramatically influence whether agents reach consensus or fragment into cliques, with implications for AI alignment and decentralized AI systems. In a three-seed mixed four-model grid, threshold-similarity routing failed to produce final behavioral or state consensus in all 189 setting-seed runs, whereas bridge-seeking routing with retained memory recovered final behavioral consensus in 14 out of 18 runs.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 15, 04:00

**Background**: Multi-agent systems involve multiple interacting intelligent agents that can solve problems collectively. The naming-game protocol is a framework where agents agree on labels for objects through local interactions. Graph feedback refers to how the structure of agent interactions (e.g., who talks to whom) influences outcomes like consensus.

## [C2TSP: Learning Tractable Near-Tour Marginals via Rooted 1-Tree](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.12127) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Researchers propose C2TSP, an end-to-end unsupervised learning pipeline that directly learns a structurally meaningful latent representation for the Traveling Salesman Problem using a connected-by-construction rooted 1-tree Gibbs family, implicit differentiation, and a smoothed Held-Karp layer for degree balance. This work shifts learning-based TSP methods from surrogate spaces to a directly interpretable Hamiltonian structure, potentially improving both performance and interpretability in combinatorial optimization. The pipeline uses implicit differentiation to learn residual edge perturbations from unbiased TSP cost, and a smoothed Held-Karp layer restores expected degree balance, with certificate-guided sharpening pushing the distribution toward tour-like structures.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 15, 04:00

**Background**: The Traveling Salesman Problem (TSP) seeks the shortest Hamiltonian cycle visiting all cities exactly once. Learning-based methods often learn surrogate representations like heatmaps or policies, leaving the Hamiltonian structure to decoding. The rooted 1-tree is a spanning tree plus an extra edge connecting the root, forming a basis for TSP relaxations. The Held-Karp algorithm is a classic dynamic programming approach for exact TSP solutions.

## [Cost-Governed RAG for Per-Tenant Attribution](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.12188) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new architecture called Cost-Governed RAG integrates a codebook-oblivious vector index (TurboVec) with a multi-tenant LLM governance gateway to achieve unified per-tenant cost attribution across retrieval and generation. Deployed on Snowpark Container Services, it attains 99.96% cost attribution accuracy across 100 simulated tenants with minimal overhead. This addresses a critical governance gap in enterprise RAG deployments where retrieval costs are typically shared and unattributed, enabling invisible cross-subsidization among tenants. The approach provides a practical solution for fair cost allocation in multi-tenant LLM systems, which is essential for enterprise adoption and compliance. The system uses TurboVec, a Rust-based vector index built on Google's TurboQuant algorithm, which provides deterministic closed-form memory formulas for near-exact per-tenant retrieval cost calculation. It reduces retrieval infrastructure cost by 3.1-9.0x compared to managed vector database services under the paper's pricing assumptions.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 15, 04:00

**Background**: In multi-tenant RAG systems, LLM generation costs are metered per token, but retrieval costs (vector memory, similarity compute, embedding API calls) are typically shared and unattributed. TurboVec uses a codebook-oblivious quantizer that requires no training, enabling deterministic cost attribution without the shared-codebook leakage risk present in trained quantizers. Snowpark Container Services allows running containerized applications within Snowflake's governance boundary.

## [4B On-Device Research Agent: Exposure Bounds Faithfulness, Retrieval Bounds Coverage](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.12257) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new study on a 4B parameter on-device research agent reveals that citation faithfulness is bounded by how much of each source the model sees (exposure), while trustworthy coverage is bounded by retrieval recall, not exposure. This work provides actionable insights for deploying small on-device research agents, showing that increasing per-source exposure is a cheap first step to improve faithfulness, and that retrieval recall is the only lever for coverage. The study used a fixed 4B generator on a 24GB laptop, crossing exposure (400 vs 1500 characters) with source quality (gold vs retrieved papers). Faithfulness improved from 0.45 to 0.58 on retrieved sources and from 0.37 to 0.58 on gold sources, converging at high exposure. Trustworthy coverage remained near 0.22 on retrieved sources due to recall held at 0.40.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 15, 04:00

**Background**: On-device research agents are small language models that search a corpus, read sources, and generate cited briefs on a personal laptop. Citation faithfulness measures whether a cited source actually supports the claim, while trustworthy coverage measures whether the agent cites the right sources. This study separates these two metrics, which are often conflated in prior work.

## [Partial Agent Benchmarks Need Varying Task Fractions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.12338) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A replay analysis of public LLM agent benchmarks (SWE-bench, AppWorld, tau-bench) reveals that partial evaluations require vastly different task fractions to match full benchmark decisions, with SWE-bench Verified needing 90% of tasks at the strictest threshold. This finding is crucial for cost-effective agent evaluation, as it shows that arbitrary partial runs can lead to incorrect conclusions, and provides guidelines for reporting partial evaluation results. At a strict 0 percentage point threshold on a 5 percentage point budget grid, AppWorld meets targets at 15% of tasks, tau-bench at 25%, SWE-bench Verified at 90%, and SWE-bench Lite does not meet all targets even at 95% under the primary coverage rule.

## [LoRA-Based Speculative Decoding Fails to Speed Up](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.12422) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new paper shows that PEFT-BD, a LoRA-based block-diffusion drafter for speculative decoding, does not achieve practical speedup despite theoretical advantages. This negative result clarifies a key condition for speculative decoding: the drafter must be substantially cheaper to execute than the verifier, guiding future research toward compute-efficient drafters. PEFT-BD uses a LoRA adapter as a block-diffusion drafter with a denoising objective, but each speculative step requires a full-backbone draft pass and a full-backbone verification pass, making the drafter compute-inefficient.

This paper uses Nancy Cartwright's definition of causes as probability raisers across contexts to define causal rules, leading to MSCRs.

Researchers propose FormalAnalyticGeo, a neural-symbolic framework that uses a formal language called CDL and SDF rendering to automatically generate multimodal analytic geometry problems, producing the AnalyticGeo7K dataset with over 7,000 verified problems. This addresses the scarcity of annotated analytic geometry data, which has hindered progress in multimodal math reasoning, and provides a scalable pipeline that eliminates human annotation effort. ## [Frozen Diffusion Model Transcribes Speech in Parallel](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.13013) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Researchers propose using a frozen discrete diffusion language model (DiffusionGemma) for automatic speech recognition, achieving parallel transcription with only 0.16% of backbone parameters trained. This work challenges the dominance of autoregressive decoders in ASR by demonstrating that discrete diffusion models can transcribe speech in parallel, potentially enabling faster and more efficient speech recognition systems. The model uses a frozen Whisper encoder and low-rank adapters to integrate audio into DiffusionGemma, achieving 6.6% word error rate on LibriSpeech test-clean with about eight parallel denoising steps. A connectionist temporal classification loss was crucial to ground the audio.

**Background**: Automatic speech recognition typically uses autoregressive models that generate tokens one by one. Discrete diffusion language models, like DiffusionGemma, generate text by iteratively denoising a sequence of random tokens in parallel, offering a faster alternative. ## [New Graded Measure for SEU Conformity in LLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.11920) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper introduces SEU sensitivity, a graded measure of an agent's conformity to subjective expected utility maximization, and validates it using Bayesian methods on LLM choice data from GPT-4o and Claude 3.5 Sonnet. This work provides a rigorous framework for evaluating decision-making under uncertainty in LLMs, which is crucial for AI alignment and safety, especially when labeled outcomes are scarce. The study uses a softmax choice model with a sensitivity parameter α, and finds that while α is identifiable given the expected-utility vector, belief and utility parameters (β, δ) are only weakly informed at realistic sample sizes.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 15, 04:00

**Background**: Subjective expected utility (SEU) maximization is a standard normative model for decision-making under uncertainty, where an agent chooses actions that maximize the expected value of a utility function based on subjective beliefs. The softmax function converts utility values into choice probabilities, with a temperature parameter controlling randomness. This paper extends that by introducing a sensitivity parameter α to measure how closely an agent follows SEU.

## [Systematic Review Exposes Data Leakage in ML for CKD Prediction](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.11963) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This systematic review of 19 studies on machine learning for early chronic kidney disease prediction introduces a taxonomy of information leakage and a quantitative leakage scoring framework, revealing that high-leakage studies report an average accuracy of 95.48% compared to 80.2% for leakage-free studies, a 15.28% inflation. This matters because inflated performance due to data leakage can mislead clinical adoption and waste resources; the proposed framework provides a standardized way to evaluate methodological reliability, potentially improving reproducibility and trust in ML-based healthcare models. The review also performed a cross-study feature stability analysis, finding that over 80% of predictors are not reliably reproducible across studies. The leakage scoring framework quantifies leakage severity, enabling systematic comparison.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 15, 04:00

**Background**: Data leakage in machine learning occurs when information from outside the training dataset inadvertently influences model training, leading to overly optimistic performance estimates. In healthcare, this is especially dangerous as it can result in models that fail in real-world deployment. Chronic kidney disease affects millions worldwide, and early detection using ML is an active research area, but methodological flaws undermine progress.

## [GSUO: Fine-Grained Guidance for Machine Unlearning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.11975) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Researchers propose GSUO, a guidance-signal-aware optimization framework that uses task-specific fine-grained signals to steer the machine unlearning process, outperforming 14 baselines in both effectiveness and efficiency. This work addresses a critical limitation in machine unlearning—the lack of precise pilot signals—which often leads to over-unlearning (harming model utility) or under-unlearning (leaving residual privacy risks). By providing differentiable guidance, GSUO enables more reliable and efficient removal of unwanted data, which is essential for privacy compliance and model management. GSUO is applicable to both random-subset and class-wise forgetting tasks, and it achieves significant speedups while maintaining high generalization. The framework designs task-specific fine-grained guidance signals to avoid uniform intervention strategies that cause over- or under-unlearning.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 15, 04:00

**Background**: Machine unlearning aims to remove the influence of specific training data from a trained model without retraining from scratch. Current methods often use global, coarse-grained strategies that treat all samples equally, ignoring that different samples have varying memorization strengths. This can lead to over-unlearning (utility loss) or under-unlearning (privacy leakage). GSUO introduces guidance signals that are aware of each sample's memorization status, enabling more precise unlearning.

## [Formalizing When Deliberate System Deficiencies Are Optimal](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.11983) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv paper formalizes when deliberately maintaining a deficiency (blind spot) in a system is economically optimal, providing conditions for removability and a coupling lemma showing limits of compensation. This work provides a rigorous economic framework for designing systems with intentional blind spots, which could impact AI safety, system design, and decision theory by shifting from minimizing deficiencies to optimizing them. The paper gives an advantage condition for when keeping a deficiency is optimal, a two-sided characterization of removability, and a coupling lemma showing that if the deficiency is a coarsening of perception, no switch can separate benefit from harm.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 15, 04:00

**Background**: In system design, deficiencies (blind spots) are typically seen as costs to minimize. This paper treats them as design variables that can be kept if they pay off and removed on demand via a compensation channel. The framework connects to Ehrlich-Becker market-vs-self-insurance and Townsend costly-state-verification theory.

A new training-free attribution method estimates the influence of upstream neurons and attention outputs on FFN neuron activations, revealing that small sparse subsets suffice to preserve activations with high fidelity. This work advances mechanistic interpretability by showing that despite dense parameters, FFN layers exhibit sparse structured dependencies, enabling scalable circuit analysis and potential efficiency gains. The method masks non-essential inputs with their average values, and when applied across all layers simultaneously, model perplexity remains largely unchanged at moderate sparsity levels.

A new paper proposes a Hierarchy-Aware RoBERTa framework for classifying cybersecurity vulnerabilities using the CWE taxonomy, demonstrating that oversampling techniques like SMOTE and ADASYN degrade deep learning performance while hierarchy-aware representation learning improves minority class F1-scores from 0.40 to 0.60. This work challenges the common practice of using oversampling for class imbalance in hierarchical text classification, offering a more principled alternative that preserves taxonomic structure, which could improve vulnerability analysis and security automation. The model achieves a weighted F1-score of 0.76 without data augmentation on a CWE Research Concept dataset, outperforming all baselines; synthetic interpolation in high-dimensional embedding spaces was found to violate parent-child constraints of the CWE hierarchy.

This paper systematically analyzes how representation spaces (CLIP and DINOv2 layers) and reference selection methods (arbitrary, semantically aligned, resynthesis-based) interact in training-free synthetic image attribution, revealing that intermediate representations yield highest accuracy and that semantically constrained references improve performance. This work provides practical guidance for building scalable attribution systems for AI-generated images without retraining, which is crucial as new image generators emerge rapidly. The findings help optimize the trade-off between accuracy and reference cost in real-world deployment. The study uses off-the-shelf pretrained representations from CLIP and DINOv2, testing three reference selection methods with varying semantic constraints. Results show that intermediate representations are not fully semantically neutral, making reference selection critical, especially under limited reference budgets.

PFAdapter introduces a hierarchical LoRA decomposition that separates adapter parameters into global-shared and local-private components for federated fine-tuning of multimodal large language models (MLLMs). This approach addresses key challenges in balancing global knowledge aggregation with local adaptation in federated learning, achieving up to 4.8% accuracy improvement and nearly 50% reduction in communication costs. The framework assigns query and key projections to global synchronization for universal multimodal semantics, while value and output projections remain localized for edge-specific adaptation, with orthogonality regularization enforcing separation.

## [SC-Flow Unifies Velocity and Endpoint Prediction in Rectified Flow](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.12171) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Researchers propose Self-Consistent Flow (SC-Flow), a method that jointly trains a single network to predict both the local velocity and the data endpoint using a lightweight consistency loss, unifying two parameterizations in rectified flow models. SC-Flow combines the training stability of endpoint prediction with the stable sampling dynamics of velocity prediction, improving generation quality and path straightness without major architectural changes, which could advance generative modeling in image synthesis and beyond. The method adds minimal computational overhead and requires no major architectural changes; extensive experiments on image generation tasks show significant gains in generation quality over standard rectified-flow baselines.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 15, 04:00

**Background**: Rectified flow models are generative models that learn a deterministic mapping from noise to data via an ordinary differential equation (ODE). The neural network can be trained to predict either the instantaneous velocity (velocity parameterization) or the data endpoint (endpoint parameterization), each with distinct advantages: endpoint prediction stabilizes training, while velocity prediction maintains stable sampling near the data manifold. SC-Flow unifies these by enforcing consistency between the two predictions.

## [Human vs. LLM Semantic Search: Temperature Tuning Falls Short](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.12195) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new study compares semantic search dynamics between humans and three LLMs (GPT-4o, Gemini-2.5-Pro, Claude-Sonnet-4.5) using verbal fluency data and trajectory-based NLP metrics, finding that humans exhibit higher entropy, larger semantic steps, and broader dispersion that cannot be fully replicated by temperature tuning alone. This research highlights fundamental differences in semantic memory retrieval between humans and LLMs, suggesting that current model architectures lack the distinctive balance between local exploitation and global exploration seen in human cognition, which has implications for improving AI's semantic understanding and search capabilities. The study analyzed 82 human participants and LLM outputs across eight temperature settings, quantifying three dimensions: entropy (step size predictability), distance to next (successive semantic steps), and distance to centroid (global dispersion). No single temperature configuration reproduced the complete human profile across all three dimensions.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 15, 04:00

**Background**: Semantic memory retrieval can be conceptualized as navigation through conceptual space. Verbal fluency tasks, where participants generate words from a category, are commonly used to study semantic search. Trajectory-based NLP metrics quantify the dynamics of this search process. Temperature is a parameter in LLMs that controls randomness in output generation.

## [Partial ID with Nonlinear Latent Regressor Measurements](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.12219) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper develops a method for linear regression with a latent regressor observed through multiple nonlinear measurements, yielding closed-form identification intervals that are robust to unknown source loadings. This provides a novel theoretical framework for handling latent regressors with nonlinear measurements, addressing a practical problem in occupational exposure measurement and offering sharp bounds that can improve inference in econometrics and related fields. The method requires at least four measurements to estimate the curvature bound via a split-instrument auxiliary regression, and Imbens-Manski confidence intervals with Stoye critical value attain uniform coverage over the curvature class.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 15, 04:00

**Background**: In linear regression, a latent regressor is an unobserved variable that influences the outcome. When it is measured with error, especially through nonlinear functions, standard regression yields biased estimates. Partial identification provides bounds on the true parameter rather than a single point estimate, which is useful when strong assumptions are not justified.

IQA-T1 introduces a framework that enhances multimodal large language models (MLLMs) for image quality assessment by integrating explicit perceptual observations from specialized analysis tools, such as noise residual maps and frequency spectra. This approach improves generalization and interpretability in open-world image quality assessment, addressing key limitations of existing MLLM-based methods that rely on semantically biased internal representations. The framework includes a new dataset, Q-Tool, with 11k multimodal reasoning chains grounded in tool-generated evidence, and achieves state-of-the-art performance across seven IQA benchmarks.

**Background**: Image Quality Assessment (IQA) aims to predict perceptual image quality aligned with human judgment. Traditional MLLM-based IQA methods use textual reasoning but are insensitive to low-level perceptual degradations due to semantic bias. IQA-T1 addresses this by incorporating tool-based visual evidence into the reasoning process.

## [ARDepth: Auto-Regressive Depth Estimation Challenges Diffusion Paradigm](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.12433) ⭐️ 7.0/10

ARDepth proposes a novel auto-regressive framework for monocular depth estimation that progressively constructs depth representations across spatial scales, using Scale-Progressive Conditioning (SPC) and Semantic-Aware Guidance (SAG) to replace the dominant diffusion-based approach. This work challenges the current diffusion paradigm in monocular depth estimation by introducing a more structured, hierarchical generation process that better reflects the piecewise and scale-dependent nature of scene geometry, potentially leading to faster and more geometrically consistent depth predictions. The model uses SPC to inject multi-scale visual features at each generation stage and SAG to provide scene-level semantic priors, enabling fine-grained local details while maintaining coherent global geometry; empirical results show strong performance across scales.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 15, 04:00

**Background**: Monocular depth estimation (MDE) aims to predict a depth map from a single image. Recently, diffusion models have become dominant for MDE by treating depth prediction as an iterative denoising process, but they assume depth is a globally smooth field, which does not explicitly model the hierarchical organization of scene geometry. ARDepth instead formulates depth estimation as auto-regressive generation, progressively constructing depth at increasing resolutions.

The method freezes the detector and only trains a lightweight traceback translator network, enabling efficient adaptation to new datasets without retraining the entire model.
