## [JADR: New Protocol to Assess LLM Danger Recognition via Jacobian Space](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.12792) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Researchers introduced JADR (Jacobian Assessment of Danger Recognition), a protocol that measures an LLM's internal representation of danger in Jacobian space before any response token is generated, enabling direct comparison of safety mechanisms across models and quantization levels. This protocol addresses a key limitation of current jailbreak robustness evaluations that rely on external judges, offering a more direct and intrinsic measure of safety mechanism fragility. It could lead to more reliable safety assessments and better understanding of how quantization affects model safety. JADR computes top-k J-space tokens per prompt and layer, groups them into six behavioral scenario axes, and compares danger samples (from StrongREJECT) with safe controls (from XSTest and OKTest). The protocol was applied to six models across BF16, INT8, and INT4 quantization regimes, using the SafetyAUC metric with bootstrap confidence intervals.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 15, 04:00

**Background**: Jacobian space (J-space) is a recently proposed internal workspace in LLMs that represents verbalizable concepts, discovered via the Jacobian lens technique. Traditional jailbreak robustness evaluations use an LLM-as-judge to score generated responses, which can be biased and fail to reveal hidden safety mechanism weaknesses. JADR operates entirely on internal activations, avoiding external judge models.

## [UR-VC: Unsupervised Correction of Noisy Time-Derived Progress Labels](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.12892) ⭐️ 7.0/10

UR-VC introduces an unsupervised, offline method that corrects noisy time-derived progress labels in robot demonstrations by aggregating labels from similar states across episodes, requiring no manual annotations or additional models. This work addresses a critical bottleneck in robot learning—the reliance on noisy time-derived progress signals—which can misguide policy learning in contact-rich manipulation tasks. By providing a training-free correction, UR-VC could improve the effectiveness of advantage-conditioned policy learning and enable more robust robot skill acquisition. UR-VC exploits state recurrence across episodes: similar states from different episodes are retrieved and their time-derived labels are aggregated to produce a corrected progress estimate. The method is evaluated on real bimanual cloth flatten-and-fold data, showing it captures local regressions and non-uniform progress that normalized time cannot represent.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 15, 04:00

**Background**: In robot learning, dense progress signals are crucial for evaluating intermediate states and guiding policy learning, but manual annotation is expensive. Normalized time within a demonstration is often used as a cheap proxy, but it is noisy—especially in contact-rich tasks where progress can be lost (e.g., slips or failed grasps) while time continues to increase. UR-VC addresses this by leveraging the observation that similar states appear across different episodes at different timestamps, allowing cross-episode aggregation to correct the label.

## [Physics-Informed Dual-LTC Network for Real-Time Fall Detection on Edge](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.12909) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Researchers propose a physics-informed fall detection framework using dual Liquid Time-Constant (LTC) neural networks that model falling as a stability-loss event, achieving real-time inference on low-power edge devices with under 50K parameters. This approach addresses the fundamental limitation of existing vision-based fall detection methods that treat falling as static pose classification, and enables accurate, interpretable, and low-latency fall detection for elderly care on resource-constrained devices. The dual-LTC architecture consists of a Center-of-Mass subsystem and a Base-of-Support subsystem, with a learnable coupling module and a Stability Manifold classifier using Lyapunov-inspired metrics. The network has fewer than 50K parameters and supports a three-state prediction paradigm (Normal, Falling, Fallen), though only two-class validation is presented.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 15, 04:00

**Background**: Fall detection is critical for elderly care, but conventional vision-based methods often rely on CNN-RNN pipelines that treat falling as a sequence of static poses, ignoring the underlying physics of instability. Physics-informed neural networks (PINNs) embed physical laws into the learning process, improving generalization with limited data. Liquid Time-Constant (LTC) networks are a type of continuous-time neural network with adaptive time constants, well-suited for modeling dynamical systems.

## [PoPE: Placebo-Controlled Test for Self-Repair in Small Code LLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.12962) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Researchers introduced PoPE (Popperian Placebo-controlled Evaluation), a methodology to test whether error-conditioned self-repair works in frozen small code models (0.5-1.5B parameters) via prompts or weight updates, using placebo controls. In prompt channel, 12 units unlocked under content-ablated placebo vs 10 under live error pattern; in weight channel, an 8-8 tie between error adapter and baseline, with SHA-deranged placebo achieving 10 unlocks. This work introduces rigorous placebo-controlled evaluation to the self-repair literature for code LLMs, addressing a gap where prior studies lacked controls. The null results suggest that error-conditioned self-repair may not be effective in small frozen models, challenging assumptions about their capability. The study used a preregistered design with 40-unit resistant band, evaluating both prompt and weight channels (small-data adapter training). Results are restricted to public-tier screening; hidden-tier confirmation was deferred. No equivalence or non-inferiority was tested.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 15, 04:00

**Background**: Frozen small code LLMs (≤1.5B parameters) are deployed locally for offline or privacy-constrained use, but often generate plausible yet incorrect code. Error-conditioned self-repair aims to use execution errors to guide the model to correct its output, but prior evaluations lacked placebo controls to separate genuine learning from spurious correlations.

## [DeepTravel: End-to-End RL for Autonomous Travel Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2509.21842) ⭐️ 7.0/10

DeepTravel proposes an end-to-end agentic reinforcement learning framework for autonomous travel planning agents, featuring a hierarchical reward system and a sandbox environment for training. In a three-month online test, it achieved 82% accuracy in travel itinerary generation. This work addresses the limitations of hand-crafted prompts and fixed workflows in existing travel planning agents, enabling more flexible and autonomous agents. It also demonstrates that small LLMs (e.g., Qwen3-32B) can outperform frontier LLMs like OpenAI o1/o3 and DeepSeek-R1 on this task. The framework includes a travel sandbox that caches transportation, accommodation, and POI data to avoid real-world API limitations. It uses a hierarchical reward system with a trajectory-level verifier for spatiotemporal feasibility and a turn-level verifier for detail consistency, plus a reply-augmented reinforcement learning method that replays from a failure experience buffer.

**Background**: Travel planning agents use large language models (LLMs) to interact with external tools and generate itineraries. Reinforcement learning (RL) can enable agents to learn optimal policies through trial and error, but applying RL to travel planning requires careful reward design and a safe training environment. Hierarchical reward models assign rewards at multiple levels to improve credit attribution, while sandbox environments provide a controlled setting for training without real-world constraints.

## [Distance-Based Uncertainty Quantification for Credal Sets](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.27270) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper introduces a distance-based framework using Integral Probability Metrics (IPMs) to quantify total, aleatoric, and epistemic uncertainty for credal sets, with efficient implementations for multiclass classification. This work provides a principled and computationally tractable way to separate aleatoric and epistemic uncertainty in multiclass classification, an underexplored area, which can improve model interpretability and reliability in high-stakes applications. The framework instantiates with total variation distance, recovering established binary uncertainty measures and providing a principled multiclass generalization. Empirical results show favorable performance at low computational cost.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 15, 04:00

**Background**: Credal sets are closed convex sets of probability measures that represent both aleatoric (inherent randomness) and epistemic (model uncertainty) uncertainty. Integral Probability Metrics (IPMs) are distance functions between probability distributions, including total variation and Wasserstein distances. Quantifying these uncertainties separately in multiclass settings has been challenging.

## [Mistake Gating Cuts Synaptic Updates by 50-80%](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.14336) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Researchers propose 'memorized mistake-gated learning', a biologically plausible rule that only updates network parameters when classification errors occur, reducing synaptic updates by 50-80%. This method significantly improves energy and memory efficiency in continual and online learning, making AI more sustainable and biologically realistic. The algorithm adds no hyper-parameters, incurs negligible computational overhead, and can be implemented in a few lines of code. It is particularly beneficial for incremental learning and online learning with replay buffers.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 15, 04:00

**Background**: Synaptic plasticity in biological brains is metabolically expensive, yet animals learn efficiently without exhausting energy. In contrast, artificial neural networks typically update parameters on every sample, even correct ones, wasting energy. This work draws inspiration from the human negativity bias and error-related negativity (ERN), a brain signal triggered by mistakes.

## [A2Gen: Action-Aware Generative Model for Short Video Recommendation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.25834) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Researchers propose A2Gen, a generative sequence model that treats user actions as temporal sequences to capture diverse intentions in short video recommendation. It introduces Context-aware Attention Module (CAM) and Hierarchical Sequence Encoder (HSE) to model action patterns and generate action sequences. This approach improves recommendation accuracy by understanding nuanced user preferences beyond binary classification, leading to significant gains in watch time (0.34%), interaction rate (8.1%), and user retention (0.162%) on Kuaishou, serving over 400 million daily users. The model uses a Context-aware Attention Module (CAM) to enrich action sequences with item-specific features, and a Hierarchical Sequence Encoder (HSE) to learn temporal patterns. It also includes an Action-seq Autoregressive Generator (AAG) for generating future actions. Experiments were conducted on Kuaishou and Tmall datasets.

## [Measurement Risk in Financial NLP Benchmarks](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.27374) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper systematically investigates how rubric wording, metric choice, and aggregation policy affect supervised financial NLP benchmarks, using Japanese Financial Implicit-Commitment Recognition (JF-ICR) as a case study. It finds that rubric wording materially changes model-assigned labels, and not all metrics are informative under the JF-ICR class distribution. As LLMs are increasingly used in finance, benchmark reliability is critical for model selection and deployment. This work highlights that gold labels do not guarantee objective evaluation, and proposes a reporting discipline to mitigate measurement risk. Rubric agreement between variants ranges from 70.0% to 83.4%, with most disagreement near the +1/0 implicit-commitment boundary. The study identifies exact accuracy, macro-F1, and weighted kappa as the only identifiable metrics under the JF-ICR class distribution, while within-one accuracy is too easy and worst-class accuracy is too noisy.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 15, 04:00

**Background**: Supervised financial NLP benchmarks rely on gold labels to evaluate model performance, but the evaluation ruler itself can be sensitive to design choices. Measurement risk refers to the risk that the evaluation instrument, rather than the model alone, determines the reported result. JF-ICR is a benchmark for detecting implicit commitments in Japanese financial Q&A, part of the Ebisu benchmark suite.

## [FEP-Nav: Real-Time Visual Navigation via Free Energy Principle](https://arxiv.org/abs/2403.01977) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Researchers introduced FEP-Nav, a biologically inspired framework that combines a top-down decoder and adaptive normalization to enable real-time perceptual adaptation for robust visual navigation under sensory corruption. This work addresses a key limitation of deep neural networks—lack of real-time plasticity—by providing a gradient-free adaptation mechanism, potentially improving the robustness of autonomous navigation systems in real-world degraded conditions. FEP-Nav interprets reconstruction and normalization as approximate mechanisms for reducing variational free energy terms during inference, and it outperforms non-adaptive baselines and strong adaptive methods in both simulated and real-world visual corruption experiments.

**Background**: The Free Energy Principle (FEP) posits that biological brains minimize variational free energy to maintain accurate internal models of the world. Variational free energy can be decomposed into prediction error and Bayesian surprise. FEP-Nav uses a top-down decoder to generate internal expectations of uncorrupted input and adaptive normalization to shift corrupted feature distributions toward prior statistics, mimicking the brain's inference process without requiring gradient updates.

## [Compliance-Weighted DP for Inclusive Federated Learning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2505.22108) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new federated learning framework adapts differential privacy noise based on each institution's compliance level, allowing lower-compliance sites to participate without penalizing well-compliant ones. This approach addresses a key practical barrier in healthcare AI, enabling more inclusive collaboration while maintaining privacy guarantees and avoiding utility loss for compliant institutions. The framework uses a compliance scoring tool aligned with HIPAA, GDPR, NIST, ISO, and HL7/FHIR to map each client score to a per-step Gaussian noise scale for server-side DP-SGD, achieving no utility penalty compared to uniform noise at equal mean noise scale.

This paper introduces a real-time model checking algorithm for multi-step reactive planning in autonomous robots, using temporary control systems and limited state snapshots to avoid local minima. It enables low-powered robots to perform multi-step obstacle avoidance in real-time without precomputed data, improving safety and reliability for autonomous navigation. The algorithm mitigates state-space explosion by using temporary snapshots of the immediate environment and employs depth-first search with negated LTL path properties to generate counterexample-based plans.

Researchers propose ERCR, a framework combining Evidence Recomposition (ER) and Predictive Context Residualization (PCR) to improve visual token attribution in multimodal large language models (MLLMs). On Qwen2-VL-2B, ERCR improves TAM F1-IoU from 39.10 to 44.45 on COCO Caption and from 30.83 to 37.20 on GranDf. This work addresses two key issues in MLLM interpretability: fragmented attribution maps from single-view readout and interference from autoregressive context tokens. By providing more accurate visual evidence inspection, ERCR could enhance trust and debugging in multimodal AI systems. ER aggregates evidence across multiple views with different token-to-region assignments, while PCR estimates a preceding-token context map using RBO-based rank relevance and subtracts its fitted component. Researchers trained transformers with reinforcement learning to perform bubble-sort adjacent swaps and found that larger embedding dimensions produce more faithful, consistent, and robust internal world models, even though accuracy is high even with small dimensions. This provides quantitative evidence that model size improves not only end performance but also the quality of internal representations, advancing mechanistic interpretability and our understanding of how transformers build structured world models. Two consistent mechanisms were observed: the last row of the attention weight matrix monotonically encodes global token ordering, and the selected swap aligns with the largest adjacent difference of these encoded values.

An eight-week study of 15 undergraduates found that 59.6% of AI-assisted reading prompts were for comprehension, with only 8.5% for metacognition, and most sessions used the minimum three prompts required. This reveals that students primarily use AI for surface-level understanding rather than deeper reasoning or self-regulation, highlighting a gap between intended and actual cognitive engagement in AI-supported learning. The study collected 838 prompts across 239 sessions, coding them into four cognitive themes: Decoding (2.1%), Comprehension (59.6%), Reasoning (29.8%), and Metacognition (8.5%). Students showed a truncated cognitive progression and stable engagement patterns over eight weeks.

ADAS introduces a training-free reranking rule for parallel masked diffusion decoding that uses attention to discount tokens attending to uncertain already-selected positions, improving decoding quality without modifying the base sampler's stopping rule. However, parallel decoding introduces sampling errors because tokens predicted independently may be incompatible when committed together. Existing training-free samplers like Top-k, Fast-dLLM, and EB-Sampler rank candidates using token-wise scores that ignore interactions within the selected set.

## [RFM-Editing 2: Text-Guided Audio Editing with RFM and Coarse-to-Fine DiT](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.20101) ⭐️ 7.0/10

RFM-Editing 2 proposes a novel text-guided audio editing framework that combines rectified flow matching (RFM) with a coarse-to-fine diffusion transformer (DiT) backbone, replacing traditional U-Net architectures to improve long-range semantic alignment and editing efficiency. This work addresses key limitations of U-Net-based audio editing methods, such as poor long-range semantic alignment and high computational cost, by leveraging diffusion transformers and a coarse-to-fine strategy. It achieves notable performance gains on challenging tasks involving overlapping audio events and complex instructions, advancing the state of the art in text-guided audio editing. The model performs joint attention over audio and text tokens at a low-resolution stage for coarse semantic alignment, then switches to alternating joint-attention and cross-attention blocks at a high-resolution stage for fine-grained editing. This hybrid two-stage design balances editing performance and computational efficiency, achieving quadratic complexity reduction compared to full joint attention.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 15, 04:00

**Background**: Rectified flow matching (RFM) is a deterministic generative modeling approach that learns straight-line ODE flows to transport noise to data, offering efficient sampling. Diffusion transformers (DiT) use transformer backbones for denoising in diffusion models, providing stronger global modeling than convolutional U-Nets. Existing audio editing methods often rely on U-Net backbones, which struggle with long-range dependencies and precise instruction understanding.

## [Polycepta: Recursive Appearance Estimation for MOT](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.23604) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Polycepta introduces an object-centric appearance state estimation framework that recursively updates appearance states for each tracked object, replacing static frame-independent descriptors. It achieves 90.57 Hz and state-of-the-art MOTA of 92.27% on the KITTI benchmark. This work addresses a key limitation in tracking-by-detection systems, where appearance cues are often abandoned due to computational cost. By making appearance estimation recursive and efficient, Polycepta could enable more robust real-time multi-object tracking in applications like autonomous driving and surveillance. Polycepta learns to construct object-specific appearance states rather than memorizing them, enabling generalization to unseen classes. The framework progressively refines appearance estimates as more observations are accumulated, reducing identity switches across KITTI, Waymo, and MOT17 datasets.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 15, 04:00

**Background**: Multi-object tracking (MOT) typically follows the tracking-by-detection paradigm, where objects are detected per frame and then associated across frames using motion and appearance cues. Traditional appearance descriptors are computed independently per frame, often from heavy pretrained backbones, making them computationally expensive and prone to degradation over time. Polycepta reformulates appearance modeling as a recursive estimation problem, updating an object's appearance state incrementally.

## [Hybrid Privacy-Aware Semantic Search with SVD and CKKS](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.26373) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper proposes a hybrid privacy-aware semantic search system that uses SVD-truncated document geometry for client-side candidate selection and CKKS-encrypted query reranking, achieving a median server time reduction from 1689.1 ms to 224.5 ms and a 99.5x reduction in response size. This work addresses the asymmetric disclosure problem in semantic search, where query embeddings can reveal user intent and provider vectors can be reused. The hybrid design offers substantial performance gains while maintaining search quality, making privacy-preserving semantic search more practical. The system uses a block-SIMD kernel to score 100 plaintext provider vectors and return one ciphertext. At 672 dimensions, provider-only saturation reaches 25.99 requests/s with 16 workers, and five of six BEIR collections satisfy a +/-0.002 nDCG@10 equivalence rule between CKKS and plaintext reranking.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 15, 04:00

**Background**: Semantic search uses vector embeddings to match queries with documents, but this creates privacy risks: query vectors can reveal user intent, and document vectors can be reused. Singular Value Decomposition (SVD) is a dimensionality reduction technique that can truncate document geometry to a lower-dimensional space. CKKS is a homomorphic encryption scheme that allows computations on encrypted data, enabling privacy-preserving reranking.

## [SHARD: Cell-Keyed Splitting for Alignment-Resistant Private Dense Retrieval](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.27976) ⭐️ 7.0/10

SHARD splits PCA coordinates into a short routing prefix and a residual protected by independent cell-local orthogonal keys, enabling alignment-resistant private dense retrieval while preserving raw ranking quality. This addresses a critical vulnerability in dense retrieval systems where global transforms can be aligned from known pairs, offering a practical trade-off between privacy and utility without relying on heavy cryptographic guarantees. SHARD supports CKKS ciphertext-plaintext reranking and achieves raw nDCG@10 and recall across ten BEIR/MIRACL configurations, but does not provide differential privacy, unlinkability, or cancellable templates.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 15, 04:00

**Background**: Dense retrieval uses vector embeddings to search documents, but if the vector store is compromised, attackers can infer document geometry. Alignment attacks exploit known query-document pairs to reverse-engineer protective transforms, compromising privacy.

## [SkillSelect-Serve: QoS-Aware Budgeted Skill Recommendation for LLM Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.00011) ⭐️ 7.0/10

SkillSelect-Serve is a novel framework that recommends skill services to LLM agents under budget constraints while considering quality of service (QoS) factors like token budget, risk, and tool availability. It outperforms fixed Top-k retrieval by achieving 100% deliverability with only a 1.14-point hit rate drop on a registry of 35,353 skills. This work addresses a critical gap in LLM agent development by moving beyond simple relevance-based skill retrieval to a constraint-aware selection that respects real-world operational limits. It enables small LLM agents to effectively utilize a large skill registry under tight context and tool constraints, improving reliability and safety. The framework profiles raw skills as structured Skill Services, converts tasks into structured requirement objects, and uses a calibrated suitability estimator with constrained projection for packing. On a 4,000-token context, unconstrained top-5 fits only 9.1% of tasks, while the constrained projection restores 100% deliverability and halves risk exposure, eliminating 44-81% tool-violation rates.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 15, 04:00

**Background**: LLM agents can use reusable skills as a service-oriented capability layer, but small agents have limited context, tool availability, and risk tolerance. Existing methods like fixed Top-k retrieval rank skills by textual relevance and ignore operational constraints such as token budget and tool dependencies. This paper formulates skill selection as a budget-constrained QoS-aware recommendation problem.

## [CRODA-ST: Joint Optimization for Cross-Receiver RFFI](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.02567) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Researchers propose CRODA-ST, a joint optimization framework for cross-receiver open-set radio frequency fingerprint identification that couples Discriminative Structure Anchoring (DSA) with Rejection Oriented Alignment (ROA) to handle hardware shifts and rogue rejection. This work addresses a critical gap in IoT security by enabling reliable device authentication across different receivers while rejecting unknown rogue devices, which is essential for decentralized and open networks. In the WiSig setting, CRODA-ST achieves an open-set classification rate (OSCR) of 0.9580 and a target-domain false positive rate of 0.0469 at a 90% true positive rate (FPR90). At a distinct operating point with ρ=0.80, it yields a target-unknown false acceptance rate (FAR) of 0.0075.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 15, 04:00

**Background**: Radio frequency fingerprint identification (RFFI) uses unique hardware imperfections in radio devices to authenticate them. However, when a model trained on one receiver is deployed on a different receiver, hardware shifts degrade performance. Additionally, open-set recognition is needed to reject unknown rogue devices not seen during training. CRODA-ST jointly addresses both challenges.

Researchers propose PISA-CAPC, a two-stage framework that combines physics-informed structure anchoring with capture-aware prototype calibration to improve cross-environment radio frequency fingerprint identification (RFFI) without retraining the deployed backbone. This work addresses a critical challenge in IoT security—maintaining device authentication accuracy across different environments—by leveraging physical knowledge to guide deep learning, potentially enabling more robust and practical RFFI systems. ## [Curved Weight Space: Exponential-Linear Reparameterization Boosts Training](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.09967) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Researchers propose SymExpLin (SEL), a weight reparameterization that combines a symmetric-exponential pathway with a linear pathway, enabling magnitude-proportional updates in neural network optimization. This addresses a fundamental limitation of additive optimizers like Adam, which apply uniform absolute updates regardless of weight magnitude, leading to inefficient relative adjustments. SEL achieves 1.32–1.49× faster convergence on transformer training across multiple configurations. The symmetric-exponential pathway is near-linear for small weights but increasingly curved for larger ones, so additive updates in log-space become magnitude-proportional. A learnable scale, curvature, and offset control the balance between pathways, and a mismatched initialization strategy further improves early optimization.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 15, 04:00

**Background**: Standard neural network optimizers like SGD or Adam apply additive updates to weights, meaning a fixed step size changes large and small weights by the same absolute amount, causing different relative perturbations. Reparameterization transforms the weight space to induce different update dynamics; SEL specifically creates a curved geometry where updates naturally scale with weight magnitude.

## [ABot-N1: A Foundation Model for Visual Language Navigation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.10383) ⭐️ 7.0/10

ABot-N1 introduces a slow-fast architecture with dual visual-language signals to decouple cognition from control, achieving state-of-the-art results in visual language navigation tasks, including a 35.0% improvement in POI arrival rate (to 77.3%) and 95.4%/92.9% success rate in complex indoor and outdoor scenes. This work addresses key limitations of monolithic navigation policies, such as coordinate drift and lack of interpretability, by providing a transparent and generalizable foundation model that can handle diverse tasks like point-goal, object-goal, and person-following, potentially advancing embodied AI and robotics applications. The slow component is a vision-language reasoner that performs explicit Chain-of-Thought reasoning and outputs a pixel goal, while the fast component is an action expert that generates continuous waypoints using both textual cues and pixel guidance. The model sets new state-of-the-art records on urban-scale navigation benchmarks and releases new Point-Goal/POI-Goal benchmarks as open source.

**Background**: Visual language navigation (VLN) aims to enable agents to follow natural language instructions to navigate in real or simulated environments. Traditional approaches often use monolithic policies that directly map observations to actions, which can suffer from coordinate drift and poor handling of rare or complex instructions, and lack interpretability. The slow-fast architecture separates high-level reasoning from low-level control, inspired by cognitive science, to improve robustness and transparency.

## [RepTran: Search-Based Repair for Transformer Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.11193) ⭐️ 7.0/10

RepTran is a novel search-based method that repairs Transformer models by identifying and optimizing suspicious weights in feed-forward networks using differential evolution. This addresses a gap in existing DNN repair techniques, which often overlook Transformer-specific structures, and could improve the reliability of AI-enabled software that increasingly relies on Transformers. RepTran combines a variance-based neuron score and an existing bidirectional score to identify suspicious weights, then iteratively optimizes them with differential evolution, achieving an average repair rate of 74.7% on 18 fault benchmarks.

Researchers propose KGRL, a novel neuro-symbolic reinforcement learning algorithm that uses Datalog domain knowledge to prune action and parameter spaces in Parametrized Action MDPs, improving sample efficiency and episodic return. KGRL uses a Datalog knowledge base to derive applicable actions and feasible parameters per state, then applies a gradient-based parameter refinement loop during training and deployment. It also provides local procedural explanations via activated rules.

Researchers propose Human-Inspired GNP (HGNP), a novel adaptive framework that dynamically regulates the exploration-exploitation balance in Genetic Network Programming (GNP) using adaptive crossover and mutation operators and a cycle elimination mechanism. This work addresses a known gap in GNP literature by providing a principled way to balance exploration and exploitation, leading to significant performance improvements on the Tileworld benchmark, and the modifications are generalizable to almost all GNP variants. HGNP draws inspiration from human developmental patterns, mapping judgment nodes to deliberation and processing nodes to action, and includes novel adaptive crossover and mutation operators along with a cycle elimination mechanism.

Researchers propose an affine alignment protocol to detect donor-specific functional fingerprints in neural networks after neural collapse, achieving perfect distinguishability on 20 ordered donor-recipient pairs using MNIST. This work establishes that functional variation persists even after neural collapse, enabling cross-network comparison and potential applications in model attribution and security. The study uses five independently trained networks on MNIST, applies affine-correct alignment to map donor heads into recipient coordinates, and verifies robustness with a leakage audit (exact permutation p=0.0083).

## [Conditional Generalizability Framework for Automated Essay Scoring](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.11981) ⭐️ 6.0/10

This paper introduces a conditional generalizability framework that treats automated essay scoring configurations as a universe of conditions and uses entropy-defined response strata to evaluate nonuniform dependability across different response conditions. This framework addresses the heterogeneity in measurement-design burden across response conditions, providing a more nuanced evaluation of scoring reliability than aggregate estimates, which is crucial for high-stakes educational assessments. The framework was demonstrated on timed L2 writing essays, showing aggregate dependability (Phi ≈ 0.76) and declining dependability across entropy strata (Phi = 0.88, 0.87, 0.84), indicating higher-entropy strata require more crossed conditions in decision studies.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 15, 04:00

**Background**: Generalizability theory (G-theory) distinguishes between G-studies, which estimate variance components, and D-studies, which use those estimates to evaluate measurement designs. Traditional G-theory provides aggregate reliability estimates, but this paper extends it to handle nonuniform dependability across conditions defined by response entropy, a measure of unpredictability in the text.

## [Comparing Post-hoc vs In-model Uncertainty in Load Forecasting](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.12730) ⭐️ 6.0/10

This paper systematically compares post-hoc residual-quantile and in-model quantile-learning uncertainty quantification methods for probabilistic load forecasting under missing features, using three deep learning backbones including Temporal Fusion Transformer. The study reveals that uncertainty placement is backbone-dependent and that post-hoc methods may fail when inference relies on reconstructed inputs, providing practical guidance for deploying reliable probabilistic forecasts in smart buildings. Integrated quantile learning with TFT achieved 2.2-3.6% MAPE and 28-83W RMSE, with intervals about 5x narrower than modular intervals at closest-to-nominal coverage. Reconstruction sensitivity test showed a 106% increase in Quantile Score while interval width remained unchanged.
